{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-13-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/.claude/skills/build-service-operations\n\n## Orient yourself first\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- app inspect --json\n```\n\nRead `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]`, in that order. Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n\nIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n\n**Background, where they exist:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-018 and its addenda, ADR-019 with addendum 1), `docs/SERVICE_OPERATIONS.md`, `docs/MODULE_EVOLUTION.md` and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. They are the deeper source for the rules below, not a prerequisite for them — the rules stand on their own.\n\n## It is not a contract, and the name is the guardrail\n\n1. A **ServiceCoverage** is operational evidence. Nobody signed it, it has no envelope, signer or artifact, and it neither amends nor replaces the Commercial Contract it was activated from. Every document, every action description and every screen says so before it says anything else.\n2. Never introduce a record, field or label called *service contract*. Anything called *Contract* here is read as signed, and then two records claim to be authoritative.\n3. `activate-service` returns `amendedCommercialRecord: false` because that is the claim a reader would otherwise make. Keep claims like it, and keep them honest.\n\n## Reach the sale only through the capability\n\n1. Service depends on `contracts/service-obligations@1` and on nothing else in that package: no import from `packages/contracts/src`, no table, no source key. If the capability lacks something, add it **inside `packages/contracts`**, never in the kernel.\n2. Open it with your own `modules`, `actor` and `now`, so the obligation update commits inside your transaction. An unawaited managed write would escape it — await every one.\n3. Consume exactly the obligations you planned, by id. Delivery obligations are not Service's business: never touch them.\n4. Evolving a shipped contracts record is ADR-019 work: bump `revision`, one step, additive. Prove it against a project that already holds real rows — an evolution that only works on an empty table is not an evolution.\n\n## Decide an entitlement, never default one\n\n1. A **service activation policy** is versioned, fingerprinted and pure. It reads the obligation and the contract and returns a support tier, covered categories and priorities, elapsed-minute targets and an optional open-case limit — or `ambiguous`.\n2. `ambiguous` blocks activation until a human supplies an override **with a stated reason**. A defaulted support tier is a promise nobody in the business made.\n3. `activate-service` recomputes the plan server-side. A caller may choose the policy and resolve an ambiguity; it never supplies the outcome.\n4. `plan-service-activation` is read-only and open to an agent: no record, no audit row, no event. Keep it that way, and test the zero counts rather than asserting them in prose.\n\n## Enforce entitlements on the server\n\n1. Category, priority, coverage state, effective dates and the open-case limit are all checked in the action. None of it may depend on the UI having filtered anything.\n2. Count the open-case limit with an **unpaged** read (`listWhere`/`countWhere`). A limit computed from a page bound lets the eleventh case through on a project with a large history.\n3. Exceeding a limit **refuses the case**. Nothing is billed for exceeding it, because nothing here bills.\n\n## SLA is elapsed time, and says so\n\n1. `firstResponseDueAt = openedAt + firstResponseTargetMinutes`, `resolutionDueAt = openedAt + resolutionTargetMinutes`. Elapsed wall-clock minutes against the injected UTC clock.\n2. There is no business-hours calendar, no holiday table, no timezone interpretation and **no paused clock**: `waiting_customer` does not stop it. A half-implemented pause understates a breach, and that is the direction that hurts.\n3. `breached` is an elapsed-time state, never a contractual or legal determination. Never write \"contractually breached\", and never emit an event that says it.\n4. `preview-sla` computes and writes nothing. `record-sla-evaluation` stores the judgement **with every input it used**, so a stored evaluation is evidence about a stated instant and never current truth.\n\n## A case is evidence, not authentication\n\n1. Every case, note and reply is what a **user actor** recorded. No customer is authenticated, no channel is integrated, and nothing is emailed, called or messaged. The `source` field is a label; a listed channel does not mean a provider is connected.\n2. `visibility` is an assertion by whoever recorded it, never an access grant.\n3. An escalation is recorded evidence that a human escalated. Nothing is routed, notified, assigned or scheduled, so a target is a label rather than a permission.\n4. Moves follow the declared transition table and nothing else. Nothing moves on a clock.\n\n## Keys, and the collisions they must not have\n\n1. A caller's key and a key the framework writes itself must live in **separate namespaces**. `sourceKey` is unique, and evidence records are append-only: a caller who seizes the key an action owns blocks that action permanently, with no correction path.\n2. Framework evidence for a repeatable move needs an **occurrence ordinal**, not just `<from>:<to>`. `in_progress ↔ waiting_customer` is the ordinary support loop, and a key that repeats collides with its own earlier evidence and strands the case.\n3. A retry answers from storage only after proving it is the same call — including the policy the caller *stated*. A divergent retry reported as honoured is worse than a refusal.\n\n## Human-only writes\n\nActivating, ending, recording a case, a first response, a transition, an activity, an SLA evaluation and an escalation each require `actor.type === 'user'`; an agent is refused `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, and the refusal writes nothing. This is a **human-actor boundary, not Service Manager or customer role enforcement** — there are no roles to enforce until the Production Spine exists. Say that wherever the boundary appears.\n\n## The Admin section\n\n`apps/admin/public/admin-service.js` is **package-scoped, not package-owned**: AX1 publishes `ADMIN_EXTENSIONS_UNSUPPORTED`, so it lives in the Admin app and renders only while `/api/schema` publishes `domains.service`. A control appears only where the server would accept it, every refusal re-throws so the parent's re-render cannot paint over it, and selection that must survive a write lives outside the render closure.\n\n## Prove it, do not argue it\n\nFault injection after every write in each action's write graph · two connections racing every decision, including the *same* move · exact reads past 500 rows · exact audit, event and trace counts · the SLA arithmetic at its exact boundaries · hostile input in every field · AX1 visibility, AX2 citability and a clean detach · a live upgrade over data that predates the change. Finish with `npm run verify`.\n\n## Never build here\n\nBilling, invoicing, payment or billing eligibility · a second legal contract or any contract amendment · renewal or expansion · an authenticated customer or agent portal · RBAC or tenancy · email, chat, WhatsApp or telephony · a contact centre · attachment or file storage · a knowledge base · business-hours calendars · a scheduler, automatic escalation or notification · customer-success health scoring.\n\n\nARGUMENTS: Design the data model for tracking support tickets and response time (first response / SLA), for daniele.pelleri@gmail.com. This is a design-only request for now — do not implement yet unless asked. Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n72\t`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n73\t\n74\t> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n75\t> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n76\t> reliability or evidence.\n77\t\n78\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n79\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n80\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n81\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n82\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n83\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n84\t- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n85\t- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n86\t\n87\t## Code review rules\n88\t\n89\t- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n90\t- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n91\t- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n92\t- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n93\t- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n94\t- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n95\t- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate above.\n96\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"e41f9393-7e0f-4f0a-840c-bc271e2da47f","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:59:12.944Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-13-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/AGENTS.md","content":"# Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n\n## Parallel coding agents\n\nSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\nany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n\n1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n   lands in another agent's tree.\n2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n\nSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n\n## Coding conventions\n\n- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- behavior works end-to-end;\n- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n- trace and audit are visible;\n- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n\n## The DX Simplicity Gate\n\nBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\nanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n\n> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n> reliability or evidence.\n\n- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n\n## Code review rules\n\n- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate above.\n","numLines":96,"startLine":1,"totalLines":96}}}

Removing the one static import in\n27\t`packages/domains/generated/index.js` removes the domain, and everything else\n28\tkeeps working — including `service-obligations@1`, which contracts goes on\n29\tproviding to nobody.\n30\t\n31\t## What ships\n32\t\n33\t```text\n34\tservice-coverage        planned → active → ended\n35\tservice-entitlement     immutable: what one obligation entitles the customer to\n36\tservice-activation-run  immutable: which obligations were consumed, under which policy\n37\tsupport-case            new → in_progress → waiting_customer → resolved → closed\n38\tsupport-case-activity   append-only: notes, transitions, recorded replies, escalations\n39\tservice-sla-evaluation  immutable: an elapsed-time judgement with the inputs it used\n40\tservice-escalation      immutable: a human recorded that this was escalated\n41\t```\n42\t\n43\tTen actions, on the records they belong to:\n44\t\n45\t```text\n46\tcommercial-contract.plan-service-activation   read-only; entitlement enforced server-side\n50\tsupport-case.record-first-response            human-only; stamped exactly once\n51\tsupport-case.transition-case                  human-only; over a declared table\n52\tsupport-case.record-case-activity             human-only; append-only\n53\tsupport-case.preview-sla                      read-only; an agent may call it\n54\tsupport-case.record-sla-evaluation            human-only; evidence about an instant\n55\tsupport-case.record-escalation                human-only; routes and notifies nothing\n56\t```\n57\t\n58\tThree read-only capabilities: `service-coverage@1`,\n59\t`service-case-management@1`, `service-sla-evidence@1`.\n60\t\n61\t## Supply an activation policy\n62\t\n63\tWhat an obligation entitles a customer to is a business decision, so it lives in\n64\ta versioned, fingerprinted policy rather than in code that reads a label:\n65\t\n66\t```js\n67\tcreateServicePackage({ policies: [myServiceActivationPolicy] })\n68\t```\n69\t\n70\tA policy may answer **`ambiguous`**, and that is the interesting case: an\n71\tobligation it cannot classify refuses activation until a human supplies an\n72\toverride with a stated reason. A defaulted support tier is a promise nobody in\n73\tthe business made.\n74\t\n75\t## The SLA contract, stated exactly\n76\t\n77\t```text\n78\tfirstResponseDueAt = openedAt + firstResponseTargetMinutes\n79\tresolutionDueAt    = openedAt + resolutionTargetMinutes\n80\t```\n81\t\n82\tElapsed wall-clock minutes against the injected UTC clock. There is **no\n83\tbusiness-hours calendar, no holiday table, no timezone interpretation and no\n84\tpaused clock** — `waiting_customer` does not stop it, because a\n85\thalf-implemented pause understates a breach. A recorded evaluation stores the\n86\tinstant and the input values it used, so it is never mistaken for current truth.\n87\t\n88\t## What it does not do\n89\t\n90\tBilling, invoicing, payment or billing eligibility · a second legal contract or\n91\tany contract amendment · renewal or expansion · an authenticated customer or\n92\tagent portal · RBAC or tenancy · email, chat, WhatsApp or telephony · a contact\n93\tcentre · attachment or file storage · a knowledge base · business-hours\n94\tcalendars · a scheduler, automatic escalation or notification · customer-success\n95\thealth scoring · Analytics Studio.\n96\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"7ee069b0-af92-4065-ba17-1c57fffcc790","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:59:17.925Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-13-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/packages/service/README.md","content":"# `packages/service` — the Service domain package\n\nThe **third** domain package built under ADR-018, and the second that depends on\nanother package. M12 raised pending Service Obligations when a contract\nactivated and then stopped; this turns them into an operational support\nrelationship.\n\n**It is not a second contract.** A **Service Coverage** is operational: nobody\nsigned it, it has no envelope, signer or artifact, and it neither amends nor\nreplaces the Commercial Contract it was activated from. The concept is called\n*coverage* precisely so that nothing here reads as a signed agreement.\n\nFull guide: [`docs/SERVICE_OPERATIONS.md`](../../docs/SERVICE_OPERATIONS.md).\nAuthoring a package of your own:\n[`docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`](../../docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md).\n\n## What it needs\n\nOne declared capability, and nothing else:\n\n```text\ncontracts/service-obligations@1\n```\n\nIt imports nothing from `packages/contracts/src` and nothing from\n`packages/core/src`. Removing the one static import in\n`packages/domains/generated/index.js` removes the domain, and everything else\nkeeps working — including `service-obligations@1`, which contracts goes on\nproviding to nobody.\n\n## What ships\n\n```text\nservice-coverage        planned → active → ended\nservice-entitlement     immutable: what one obligation entitles the customer to\nservice-activation-run  immutable: which obligations were consumed, under which policy\nsupport-case            new → in_progress → waiting_customer → resolved → closed\nsupport-case-activity   append-only: notes, transitions, recorded replies, escalations\nservice-sla-evaluation  immutable: an elapsed-time judgement with the inputs it used\nservice-escalation      immutable: a human recorded that this was escalated\n```\n\nTen actions, on the records they belong to:\n\n```text\ncommercial-contract.plan-service-activation   read-only; writes nothing at all\ncommercial-contract.activate-service          human-only; consumes the obligations\nservice-coverage.end-service-coverage         human-only; closes no case, bills nothing\nservice-entitlement.record-service-case       human-only; entitlement enforced server-side\nsupport-case.record-first-response            human-only; stamped exactly once\nsupport-case.transition-case                  human-only; over a declared table\nsupport-case.record-case-activity             human-only; append-only\nsupport-case.preview-sla                      read-only; an agent may call it\nsupport-case.record-sla-evaluation            human-only; evidence about an instant\nsupport-case.record-escalation                human-only; routes and notifies nothing\n```\n\nThree read-only capabilities: `service-coverage@1`,\n`service-case-management@1`, `service-sla-evidence@1`.\n\n## Supply an activation policy\n\nWhat an obligation entitles a customer to is a business decision, so it lives in\na versioned, fingerprinted policy rather than in code that reads a label:\n\n```js\ncreateServicePackage({ policies: [myServiceActivationPolicy] })\n```\n\nA policy may answer **`ambiguous`**, and that is the interesting case: an\nobligation it cannot classify refuses activation until a human supplies an\noverride with a stated reason. A defaulted support tier is a promise nobody in\nthe business made.\n\n## The SLA contract, stated exactly\n\n```text\nfirstResponseDueAt = openedAt + firstResponseTargetMinutes\nresolutionDueAt    = openedAt + resolutionTargetMinutes\n```\n\nElapsed wall-clock minutes against the injected UTC clock. @ts-check\n\nimport { definePackage } from '../../core/index.js';\nimport { buildServiceActions, serviceMetadata } from './actions.js';\nimport { SERVICE_POLICY_KIND, defineServiceActivationPolicy } from './activation-policy.js';\nimport {\n  REQUIRED_CAPABILITY, SERVICE_PACKAGE, WORK_FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY, servicePolicyFingerprint,\n} from './service-actions.js';\nimport {\n  createCaseManagementCapability, createCoverageCapability, createSlaEvidenceCapability,\n} from './capabilities.js';\n\n/**\n * The Service domain package — the **third** built under ADR-018, and the\n * second that depends on another package.\n *\n * M12 raised pending Service Obligations when a contract activated and then\n * stopped. This turns them into an operational support relationship: a Service\n * Coverage with its Entitlements, support cases over an explicit transition\n * table, elapsed-time SLA evidence, and manually recorded escalation.\n *\n * **It is not a second contract.** A Service Coverage is operational: nobody\n * signed it, it has no envelope, signer or artifact, and it neither amends nor\n * replaces the Commercial Contract it was activated from. The concept is named\n * *coverage* precisely so that nothing here reads as a signed agreement.\n *\n * Its only reach into another package is the declared capability\n * `contracts/service-obligations@1`. It imports nothing from\n * `packages/contracts/src` and nothing from `packages/core/src`. Removing the\n * one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js` removes the\n * domain, and everything else keeps working.\n */\n\nexport { SERVICE_PACKAGE };\nexport const SERVICE_RESOURCES = Object.freeze([\n  'service-coverage', 'service-entitlement', 'service-activation-run',\n  'support-case', 'support-case-activity', 'service-sla-evaluation', 'service-escalation',\n]);\n\n/**\n * @param {{policies?: any[], modules?: Record<string, string>, followUp?: boolean}} [options]\n *   `policies` are service activation policy definitions (validated here);\n *   `modules` overrides record-module names for a project that renamed them;\n *   `followUp: true` makes this package a consumer of `work/follow-up@1`, so a\n *   recorded escalation also opens one work task in the same transaction. It is\n *   opt-in because `requires` is hard and every existing composition must keep\n *   booting unchanged (Work v1, ADR-030).\n */\nexport function createServicePackage(options = {}) {\n  const followUp = options.followUp === true;\n  const policies = (options.policies ?? []).map((definition) => ({\n    kind: SERVICE_POLICY_KIND,\n    definition: defineServiceActivationPolicy(definition),\n  }));\n\n  return definePackage({\n    packageContract: 1,\n    name: SERVICE_PACKAGE,\n    label: 'Service operations',\n    version: 1,\n    description: 'Activates a contract\\'s pending service obligations into an operational Service Coverage with immutable Entitlements, and records support cases, elapsed-time SLA evidence and manual escalation. It is not a signed agreement, it amends no commercial record, and it bills, sends and schedules nothing.',\n    // The one declared reach into another package. Without it this package\n    // refuses to register: it cannot invent the obligations it activates.\n    requires: followUp\n      ? [{ ...REQUIRED_CAPABILITY }, { ...WORK_FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY }]\n      : [{ ...REQUIRED_CAPABILITY }],\n    capabilities: [\n      createCoverageCapability({ modules: options.modules }),\n      createCaseManagementCapability({ modules: options.modules }),\n      createSlaEvidenceCapability({ modules: options.modules }),\n    ],\n    resources: [...SERVICE_RESOURCES],\n    actions: buildServiceActions({ modules: options.modules, policies, followUp }),\n    policies,\n    /** Function-free, additive schema metadata — never a handler or a secret. */\n    metadata() {\n      return {\n        ...serviceMetadata(policies),\n        workFollowUp: followUp\n          ? 'composed with followUp enabled: recording an escalation also opens exactly one work task on that escalation, through work/follow-up@1, in the same transaction. Nothing is still routed, notified, assigned or scheduled by it, and the task carries no due date'\n          : 'not composed. Without followUp enabled this package requires nothing from work, creates no task, and behaves exactly as it did before Work v1',\n        policyFingerprints: policies.map(({ definition }) => ({\n          name: definition.name,\n          version: definition.version,\n          fingerprint: servicePolicyFingerprint(definition),\n        })),\n      };\n    },\n  });\n}\n\nexport { defineServiceActivationPolicy, SERVICE_POLICY_KIND, CASE_PRIORITIES, AMBIGUOUS } from './activation-policy.js';\nexport {\n  CASE_STATES, CASE_TRANSITIONS, COVERAGE_STATES, ESCALATION_LEVELS, SLA_STATES,\n  ACTIVITY_TYPES, VISIBILITY, AT_RISK_FRACTION, evaluateSla, addMinutes, servicePolicyFingerprint,\n} from './service-actions.js';\nexport { serviceMetadata } from './actions.js';\nexport {\n  COVERAGE_CAPABILITY, CASE_CAPABILITY, SLA_CAPABILITY,\n} from './capabilities.js';\n","numLines":101,"startLine":1,"totalLines":101}}}

Here's the data model, as built.\n\n## Records\n\n**`ServiceCoverage`** — the operational relationship a case hangs off (not a contract; nobody signs it)\n`contractId`, `orderId`, `obligationRefs`, `customerRef`/`companyId`/`contactId`, `status` (`planned → active → ended`), `startDate`/`endDate`, `policyName`/`policyVersion`/`policyFingerprint`, `entitlementCount`, `activatedBy`/`activatedAt`, `endedReason`/`endedBy`/`endedAt`.\n\n**`ServiceEntitlement`** *(immutable)* — what one obligation entitles the customer to: tier, categories, targets\n`serviceCoverageId`, `contractId`, `serviceObligationId`, `supportTier`, `categories`, `priorities`, `firstResponseTargetMinutes`, `resolutionTargetMinutes`, `maxOpenCases`, `quantity`, `startDate`/`endDate`, `policyName`/`policyVersion`/`policyFingerprint`, `decisionReason`, `createdBy`/`issuedAt`.\n\n**`ServiceActivationRun`** *(immutable)* — evidence of the activation event itself\n`contractId`, `serviceCoverageId`, `obligationRefs`/`obligationCount`, `entitlementCount`, `policyName`/`policyVersion`/`policyFingerprint`, `overrideCount`, `activatedBy`/`activatedAt`.\n\n**`SupportCase`** — the ticket\n`caseNumber`, `serviceCoverageId`, `serviceEntitlementId`, `contractId`, `customerRef`/`customerContactRef`, `source` (`manual`/`api`), `category`, `priority` (`low/normal/high/urgent`), `title`, `description`, `status` (`new → in_progress → waiting_customer → resolved → closed`), `ownerRef`, `openedBy`/`openedAt`, **SLA timestamps**: `firstResponseDueAt`, `resolutionDueAt`, `firstRespondedAt`, `resolvedAt`, plus `resolutionSummary`, `closedAt`.\n\n**`SupportCaseActivity`** *(append-only)* — every note, transition, reply, escalation on a case\n`supportCaseId`, `serviceCoverageId`, `type` (`note/transition/customer_reply/internal_note/escalation`), `body`, `evidenceRef`, `visibility` (`internal/customer_visible`), `fromStatus`/`toStatus`, `recordedBy`/`occurredAt`.\n\n**`ServiceSlaEvaluation`** *(immutable)* — a stamped judgement of elapsed-time SLA state, with its inputs\n`supportCaseId`, `serviceCoverageId`, `serviceEntitlementId`, `firstResponseState`/`resolutionState` (`met/on_track/at_risk/breached/not_applicable`), `evaluatedAt`, and the exact inputs used: `openedAt`, `firstResponseDueAt`, `resolutionDueAt`, `firstRespondedAt`, `resolvedAt`, `basis`, `recordedBy`.\n\n**`ServiceEscalation`** *(immutable)* — a human recorded that a case was escalated\n`supportCaseId`, `serviceCoverageId`, `level` (`internal/management/vendor`), `reason`, `targetRef`, `slaEvaluationId`, `evidenceRef`, `recordedBy`/`recordedAt`.\n\n## How the pieces connect\n\n```\nContract ──activate-service──▶ ServiceCoverage ──▶ ServiceEntitlement (per obligation, immutable)\n                                      │                       │\n                                      ▼                       ▼\n                              ServiceActivationRun     SupportCase (against one entitlement)\n                                                                │\n                                                ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐\n                                                ▼               ▼               ▼\n                                    SupportCaseActivity  ServiceSlaEvaluation  ServiceEscalation\n                                     (append-only log)   (immutable snapshot)  (immutable evidence)\n```\n\n## The response-speed math\n\n```\nfirstResponseDueAt = openedAt + firstResponseTargetMinutes   (from the entitlement)\nresolutionDueAt    = openedAt + resolutionTargetMinutes\n```\n\nPure elapsed wall-clock minutes against an injected UTC clock — no business-hours calendar, no holidays, no pause while `waiting_customer`. `preview-sla` computes this live and writes nothing; `record-sla-evaluation` stamps a permanent snapshot with every input it used, so a stored evaluation is evidence about one instant, never \"current truth.\"\n\n## Guardrails baked into the design\n\n- **Entitlement is decided, never defaulted** — a versioned policy classifies each obligation into tier/categories/targets, or returns `ambiguous` and blocks activation until a human overrides with a stated reason.\n- **Open-case limits are enforced server-side** via an unpaged count, so a customer can't exceed their entitlement by opening enough cases to push the offender past a page boundary.\n- **All mutations are human-only** (`actor.type === 'user'`) — an agent can only call the two read-only actions (`plan-service-activation`, `preview-sla`).\n- **Coverage ≠ contract**: no envelope, signer or artifact; it neither amends nor replaces the Commercial Contract.\n- **No** email/chat/telephony integration, no attachment storage, no auto-escalation/notification, no business-hours calendar — `source`, `visibility`, and escalation `targetRef` are labels, not integrations or permissions.\n\nSince this is already built and tested (`tests/service-operations-*.test.js`), the real question is whether you want to **use it as-is** (e.g. via `commercial-contract.activate-service` off an existing contract) or whether you actually need something *not* tied to a Commercial Contract/obligation — e.g. standalone tickets with no entitlement behind them. (`internal/management/vendor`), `reason`, `targetRef`, `slaEvaluationId`, `evidenceRef`, `recordedBy`/`recordedAt`.\n\n## How the pieces connect\n\n```\nContract ──activate-service──▶ ServiceCoverage ──▶ ServiceEntitlement (per obligation, immutable)\n                                      │                       │\n                                      ▼                       ▼\n                              ServiceActivationRun     SupportCase (against one entitlement)\n                                                                │\n                                                ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐\n                                                ▼               ▼               ▼\n                                    SupportCaseActivity  ServiceSlaEvaluation  ServiceEscalation\n                                     (append-only log)   (immutable snapshot)  (immutable evidence)\n```\n\n## The response-speed math\n\n```\nfirstResponseDueAt = openedAt + firstResponseTargetMinutes   (from the entitlement)\nresolutionDueAt    = openedAt + resolutionTargetMinutes\n```\n\nPure elapsed wall-clock minutes against an injected UTC clock — no business-hours calendar, no holidays, no pause while `waiting_customer`. `preview-sla` computes this live and writes nothing; `record-sla-evaluation` stamps a permanent snapshot with every input it used, so a stored evaluation is evidence about one instant, never \"current truth.\"\n\n## Guardrails baked into the design\n\n- **Entitlement is decided, never defaulted** — a versioned policy classifies each obligation into tier/categories/targets, or returns `ambiguous` and blocks activation until a human overrides with a stated reason.\n- **Open-case limits are enforced server-side** via an unpaged count, so a customer can't exceed their entitlement by opening enough cases to push the offender past a page boundary.\n- **All mutations are human-only** (`actor.type === 'user'`) — an agent can only call the two read-only actions (`plan-service-activation`, `preview-sla`).\n- **Coverage ≠ contract**: no envelope, signer or artifact; it neither amends nor replaces the Commercial Contract.\n- **No** email/chat/telephony integration, no attachment storage, no auto-escalation/notification, no business-hours calendar — `source`, `visibility`, and escalation `targetRef` are labels, not integrations or permissions.\n\nSince this is already built and tested (`tests/service-operations-*.test.js`), the real question is whether you want to **use it as-is** (e.g. via `commercial-contract.activate-service` off an existing contract) or whether you actually need something *not* tied to a Commercial Contract/obligation — e.g. standalone tickets with no entitlement behind them.
